Well, I guess the main thing is my thinking feels… off. Like my head is foggy, and I’m not usually like that. I work as a personal care aide, so I’m used to keeping track of details and writing things down clearly, but lately I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, I don’t think, but enough that I notice it and it bothers me.

And I’ll start to do something, like I’m in the middle of a task, and then I just kind of blank on what I was about to do. It’s frustrating because I’m 43, I know my routines, I know my work, and I’m usually pretty on top of things. But now it feels like I’m moving through the day with this haze in my head, and I don’t trust myself as much with the small stuff. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.

Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve still been getting up and going to work, and I can talk to my clients and do what I’m supposed to do, so from the outside I probably seem okay. But inside it’s been different.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.

And then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.

I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.

It’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”

  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.

Like with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.

It’s not even always like I’m crying or anything dramatic, it’s more like this sad, flat feeling that’s just sitting there. I can push through my shift and answer people and make small talk, but it feels like I’m kind of doing it from behind a wall, if that makes sense. Like I’m present, but inside I’m just… low. Sad and kind of hollow.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed some big thing where I suddenly hate everything I used to like. It’s more that when I do things, I’m kind of not fully in them, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I’ll still put on a show or scroll on my phone or talk to someone if they call, but I don’t always get much out of it. But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Oh, I don’t enjoy anything anymore.” It’s more… I don’t know. Things feel a little muted, maybe. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.

I keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.

It’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.

But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.

At work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.

And at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.

I work as a personal care aide, so I’m used to keeping track of details and writing things down clearly, but lately I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, I don’t think, but enough that I notice it and it bothers me.\n\nAnd I’ll start to do something, like I’m in the middle of a task, and then I just kind of blank on what I was about to do. It’s frustrating because I’m 43, I know my routines, I know my work, and I’m usually pretty on top of things. But now it feels like I’m moving through the day with this haze in my head, and I don’t trust myself as much with the small stuff. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve still been getting up and going to work, and I can talk to my clients and do what I’m supposed to do, so from the outside I probably seem okay. But inside it’s been different.\n\nLately when I wake up, there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s not even always like I’m crying or anything dramatic, it’s more like this sad, flat feeling that’s just sitting there. I can push through my shift and answer people and make small talk, but it feels like I’m kind of doing it from behind a wall, if that makes sense. Like I’m present, but inside I’m just… low. Sad and kind of hollow.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed some big thing where I suddenly hate everything I used to like. It’s more that when I do things, I’m kind of not fully in them, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I’ll still put on a show or scroll on my phone or talk to someone if they call, but I don’t always get much out of it. But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Oh, I don’t enjoy anything anymore.” It’s more… I don’t know. Things feel a little muted, maybe. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 43 / personal_care_aide / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Death_Suicide\nmanifestation: \"I’m not planning to hurt myself, but there have been mornings when I think it wouldn’t be the worst thing if I just didn’t wake up, and that scares me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not planning to hurt myself. I want to say that clearly, because even saying this out loud makes me nervous, like I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something.

But… there have been mornings where I wake up and, before I even really get going, I have this thought like, “If I just hadn’t woken up today, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” Not like I’m making a plan or thinking through how to hurt myself. It’s more passive than that, I guess. Like being tired of feeling the way I’m feeling, and then that thought shows up.

And honestly, it scares me. Because I don’t usually think like that, and then I feel guilty or ashamed for even having the thought. I haven’t done anything to harm myself, and I’m not sitting there planning it, but yeah… those thoughts have crossed my mind.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the main thing is my thinking feels… off. Like my head is foggy, and I’m not usually like that. I work as a personal care aide, so I’m used to keeping track of details and writing things down clearly, but lately I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, I don’t think, but enough that I notice it and it bothers me.\n\nAnd I’ll start to do something, like I’m in the middle of a task, and then I just kind of blank on what I was about to do. It’s frustrating because I’m 43, I know my routines, I know my work, and I’m usually pretty on top of things. But now it feels like I’m moving through the day with this haze in my head, and I don’t trust myself as much with the small stuff. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve still been getting up and going to work, and I can talk to my clients and do what I’m supposed to do, so from the outside I probably seem okay. But inside it’s been different.\n\nLately when I wake up, there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s not even always like I’m crying or anything dramatic, it’s more like this sad, flat feeling that’s just sitting there. I can push through my shift and answer people and make small talk, but it feels like I’m kind of doing it from behind a wall, if that makes sense. Like I’m present, but inside I’m just… low. Sad and kind of hollow.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed some big thing where I suddenly hate everything I used to like. It’s more that when I do things, I’m kind of not fully in them, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I’ll still put on a show or scroll on my phone or talk to someone if they call, but I don’t always get much out of it. But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Oh, I don’t enjoy anything anymore.” It’s more… I don’t know. Things feel a little muted, maybe. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. I want to say that clearly, because even saying this out loud makes me nervous, like I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something.\n\nBut… there have been mornings where I wake up and, before I even really get going, I have this thought like, “If I just hadn’t woken up today, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” Not like I’m making a plan or thinking through how to hurt myself. It’s more passive than that, I guess. Like being tired of feeling the way I’m feeling, and then that thought shows up.\n\nAnd honestly, it scares me. Because I don’t usually think like that, and then I feel guilty or ashamed for even having the thought. I haven’t done anything to harm myself, and I’m not sitting there planning it, but yeah… those thoughts have crossed my mind.\n\n[Last Question]\nOver the past two weeks, have you felt this sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. Most days, anyway. It’s been this thing where I wake up and before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I haven’t even gotten out of bed yet and it’s just… there.

I can still go to work, I can talk to people, do my shift, answer questions, whatever I need to do on the outside. So I don’t think everyone would necessarily look at me and say, “Oh, he’s sad all day.” But inside I feel really flat. Like I’m moving through the day, but there’s not much there underneath except this sadness.

And it’s not every single second, I guess. If someone talks to me, I can respond, maybe even laugh a little if I’m supposed to. But then when things get quiet again, it drops right back down. So yeah… over the past couple weeks, it’s been most of the day, nearly every day.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.21, F32.9, F51.9, F41.9, R41.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess the main thing is my thinking feels… off. Like my head is foggy, and I’m not usually like that. I work as a personal care aide, so I’m used to keeping track of details and writing things down clearly, but lately I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, I don’t think, but enough that I notice it and it bothers me.\n\nAnd I’ll start to do something, like I’m in the middle of a task, and then I just kind of blank on what I was about to do. It’s frustrating because I’m 43, I know my routines, I know my work, and I’m usually pretty on top of things. But now it feels like I’m moving through the day with this haze in my head, and I don’t trust myself as much with the small stuff. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve still been getting up and going to work, and I can talk to my clients and do what I’m supposed to do, so from the outside I probably seem okay. But inside it’s been different.\n\nLately when I wake up, there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s not even always like I’m crying or anything dramatic, it’s more like this sad, flat feeling that’s just sitting there. I can push through my shift and answer people and make small talk, but it feels like I’m kind of doing it from behind a wall, if that makes sense. Like I’m present, but inside I’m just… low. Sad and kind of hollow.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed some big thing where I suddenly hate everything I used to like. It’s more that when I do things, I’m kind of not fully in them, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I’ll still put on a show or scroll on my phone or talk to someone if they call, but I don’t always get much out of it. But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Oh, I don’t enjoy anything anymore.” It’s more… I don’t know. Things feel a little muted, maybe. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. I want to say that clearly, because even saying this out loud makes me nervous, like I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something.\n\nBut… there have been mornings where I wake up and, before I even really get going, I have this thought like, “If I just hadn’t woken up today, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” Not like I’m making a plan or thinking through how to hurt myself. It’s more passive than that, I guess. Like being tired of feeling the way I’m feeling, and then that thought shows up.\n\nAnd honestly, it scares me. Because I don’t usually think like that, and then I feel guilty or ashamed for even having the thought. I haven’t done anything to harm myself, and I’m not sitting there planning it, but yeah… those thoughts have crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt this sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days, anyway. It’s been this thing where I wake up and before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I haven’t even gotten out of bed yet and it’s just… there.\n\nI can still go to work, I can talk to people, do my shift, answer questions, whatever I need to do on the outside. So I don’t think everyone would necessarily look at me and say, “Oh, he’s sad all day.” But inside I feel really flat. Like I’m moving through the day, but there’s not much there underneath except this sadness.\n\nAnd it’s not every single second, I guess. If someone talks to me, I can respond, maybe even laugh a little if I’m supposed to. But then when things get quiet again, it drops right back down. So yeah… over the past couple weeks, it’s been most of the day, nearly every day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the main things that made me think, okay, maybe I need to talk to someone, because my head has just felt foggy lately.

It’s frustrating because I’m 43, I know my routines, I know my work, and I’m usually pretty on top of things. But now it feels like I’m moving through the day with this haze in my head, and I don’t trust myself as much with the small stuff. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve still been getting up and going to work, and I can talk to my clients and do what I’m supposed to do, so from the outside I probably seem okay. But inside it’s been different.\n\nLately when I wake up, there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s not even always like I’m crying or anything dramatic, it’s more like this sad, flat feeling that’s just sitting there. I can push through my shift and answer people and make small talk, but it feels like I’m kind of doing it from behind a wall, if that makes sense. Like I’m present, but inside I’m just… low. Sad and kind of hollow.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed some big thing where I suddenly hate everything I used to like. It’s more that when I do things, I’m kind of not fully in them, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I’ll still put on a show or scroll on my phone or talk to someone if they call, but I don’t always get much out of it. But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Oh, I don’t enjoy anything anymore.” It’s more… I don’t know. Things feel a little muted, maybe. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s not like I’m collapsing or anything, but I do feel more worn out than I’d expect. Even on days where I haven’t really done that much, my body can feel like I’ve been going all day.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or doing the usual care stuff, things I normally can handle, it just feels like it takes more out of me. And even simple things outside of work, like carrying groceries in, I’ll notice I’m more drained than I think I should be. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. I want to say that clearly, because even saying this out loud makes me nervous, like I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something.\n\nBut… there have been mornings where I wake up and, before I even really get going, I have this thought like, “If I just hadn’t woken up today, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” Not like I’m making a plan or thinking through how to hurt myself. It’s more passive than that, I guess. Like being tired of feeling the way I’m feeling, and then that thought shows up.\n\nAnd honestly, it scares me. Because I don’t usually think like that, and then I feel guilty or ashamed for even having the thought. I haven’t done anything to harm myself, and I’m not sitting there planning it, but yeah… those thoughts have crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt this sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days, anyway. It’s been this thing where I wake up and before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I haven’t even gotten out of bed yet and it’s just… there.\n\nI can still go to work, I can talk to people, do my shift, answer questions, whatever I need to do on the outside. So I don’t think everyone would necessarily look at me and say, “Oh, he’s sad all day.” But inside I feel really flat. Like I’m moving through the day, but there’s not much there underneath except this sadness.\n\nAnd it’s not every single second, I guess. If someone talks to me, I can respond, maybe even laugh a little if I’m supposed to. But then when things get quiet again, it drops right back down. So yeah… over the past couple weeks, it’s been most of the day, nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has gone down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly noticed it going down, like I’m not looking at meals and feeling repulsed by food or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel especially interested in eating, but I can’t tell if that’s an appetite thing or just me being distracted and not thinking about it until later. If food is in front of me, I usually eat. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the main things that made me think, okay, maybe I need to talk to someone, because my head has just felt foggy lately.\n\nIt’s not like I can’t understand things, exactly. It’s more like I’ll be in the middle of doing something and then suddenly I forget what I was about to do. Or I’ll read something and have to go back over it because it didn’t really stick the first time. And with my work, that worries me, because I’m usually pretty careful. I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes — nothing huge, but enough that I notice, like leaving something out or having to double-check myself because I’m not sure if I wrote it down right.\n\nThat’s not really like me. I take that stuff seriously. So when I catch myself blanking out or losing my train of thought, it makes me feel even worse, because then I start thinking, “What is wrong with me? Why can’t I just focus?” It’s been frustrating.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t know that I’ve noticed that, no. I mean… I feel slower inside, if that makes sense, like it takes me longer to get myself going or to sort through what I’m trying to do, but I’m not sure other people would say I’m moving slowly or talking slowly.

But I also don’t know if that’s “losing interest” or just being tired or distracted or in my own head. I haven’t really sat down and thought, “Oh, I don’t enjoy anything anymore.” It’s more… I don’t know. Things feel a little muted, maybe. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Not great, honestly. It’s been kind of all over the place. Some nights I get into bed and I’m tired, like I know I should be able to sleep, but then my mind just starts going. A lot of it is family stuff—little things, worries, conversations, what I should’ve done or what I need to do—and I’ll just lie there staring into the dark, kind of turning it over and over.\n\nAnd then other mornings I wake up before my alarm, not even for a clear reason, and once I’m awake I can’t really settle back down. I’ll try to tell myself, “You still have time, just go back to sleep,” but it doesn’t happen. I end up just lying there half-resting, half-thinking, and by the time I actually need to get up I already feel like I’ve been awake for a while. So yeah, it’s not like one simple pattern, but it hasn’t felt steady.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot” exactly. I mean, I do get stuck thinking about things sometimes, especially when I’m trying to settle down, but it’s hard for me to separate what’s normal worrying from something more than that.\n\nIt’s not like I’m walking around every minute panicking or imagining disasters, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like my mind picks up little things and keeps turning them over—stuff I said, things I need to do, whether I handled something right. But I don’t know… I wouldn’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s there, but I’m not sure how much counts as “a lot.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s kind of frustrating because I’ll think, “I didn’t even do enough to feel this tired,” but my body doesn’t seem to agree.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’d say it really started, or at least became noticeable, after my uncle died… so about two weeks ago now. Before that, I mean, I had normal tired days here and there like anybody, but this whole shift in how I’ve been feeling, the being worn down and not quite sleeping right, it kind of started around then. It’s been hard to tell because the days have sort of blended together a little since it happened, but when I think back, that’s the point where I noticed I wasn’t feeling like myself.\n\nDoctor: Since your uncle died, have you found yourself strongly longing for him or preoccupied with thoughts about him?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “strongly longing,” exactly. I mean, I think about him, sure. It comes up, especially if something reminds me of him, or if there’s a quiet moment and my mind wanders that way. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. I want to say that clearly, because even saying this out loud makes me nervous, like I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something.\n\nBut… there have been mornings where I wake up and, before I even really get going, I have this thought like, “If I just hadn’t woken up today, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” Not like I’m making a plan or thinking through how to hurt myself. It’s more passive than that, I guess. Like being tired of feeling the way I’m feeling, and then that thought shows up.\n\nAnd honestly, it scares me. Because I don’t usually think like that, and then I feel guilty or ashamed for even having the thought. I haven’t done anything to harm myself, and I’m not sitting there planning it, but yeah… those thoughts have crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt this sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days, anyway. It’s been this thing where I wake up and before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. But I’m not sitting there all day just focused on him, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more like… it’s there in the background sometimes. I’ll remember something about him or think, “Wow, he’s really gone,” and that feels strange. But I don’t know that I’d say I’m preoccupied. At least not in a way where I can’t think about anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… guilty, definitely. Worthless, I don’t know if I’d use that exact word, but I’ve been feeling like I let him down. My tío, I mean.\n\nI keep going back over it in my head, like, “Why didn’t I call him more?” or “Why did I keep putting off visiting?” And I know people say you can’t think like that, you don’t always know what’s going to happen, but it doesn’t really stop my brain from doing it. I’ll remember times I thought about reaching out and then got busy or tired or said, “I’ll do it later,” and now there isn’t a later. That part really gets to me.\n\nIt’s like I failed him as family somehow. Like I should’ve been more present, checked in more, made more effort before he died. I don’t know if that’s fair, but it’s where my mind keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m still showing up and doing what I’m supposed to do, so it’s not like everything has completely fallen apart, but I can tell I’m not as sharp with the little details.\n\nAt work, because I do care work, there’s paperwork and notes for clients, and usually I’m pretty careful with that. Lately I’ve been second-guessing myself more, like, “Did I write that down right? Did I miss something?” So I’ve had to ask coworkers to look over my documentation, just to make sure I didn’t mess anything up. That’s embarrassing for me, honestly, because I don’t like feeling like people have to babysit my work.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has picked up some of the bills and scheduling stuff, like appointments or things we need to keep track of. Normally I can manage my share of that, but right now I’m just not as reliable with details. I’ll forget to follow through or I’ll sit there staring at something and not really process it. So yeah, it’s affecting things, not severely, but enough that other people are having to compensate for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not planning to hurt myself. I want to say that clearly, because even saying this out loud makes me nervous, like I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something.\n\nBut… there have been mornings where I wake up and, before I even really get going, I have this thought like, “If I just hadn’t woken up today, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” Not like I’m making a plan or thinking through how to hurt myself. It’s more passive than that, I guess. Like being tired of feeling the way I’m feeling, and then that thought shows up.\n\nAnd honestly, it scares me. Because I don’t usually think like that, and then I feel guilty or ashamed for even having the thought. I haven’t done anything to harm myself, and I’m not sitting there planning it, but yeah… those thoughts have crossed my mind.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt this sad or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days, anyway. It’s been this thing where I wake up and before anything has even happened, there’s already this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like I haven’t even gotten out of bed yet and it’s just… there.\n\nI can still go to work, I can talk to people, do my shift, answer questions, whatever I need to do on the outside. So I don’t think everyone would necessarily look at me and say, “Oh, he’s sad all day.” But inside I feel really flat. Like I’m moving through the day, but there’s not much there underneath except this sadness.\n\nAnd it’s not every single second, I guess. If someone talks to me, I can respond, maybe even laugh a little if I’m supposed to. But then when things get quiet again, it drops right back down. So yeah… over the past couple weeks, it’s been most of the day, nearly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your appetite has gone down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly noticed it going down, like I’m not looking at meals and feeling repulsed by food or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel especially interested in eating, but I can’t tell if that’s an appetite thing or just me being distracted and not thinking about it until later. If food is in front of me, I usually eat. I haven’t been tracking it closely, though, so I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely. That’s actually one of the main things that made me think, okay, maybe I need to talk to someone, because my head has just felt foggy lately.\n\nIt’s not like I can’t understand things, exactly. It’s more like I’ll be in the middle of doing something and then suddenly I forget what I was about to do. Or I’ll read something and have to go back over it because it didn’t really stick the first time. And with my work, that worries me, because I’m usually pretty careful. I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes — nothing huge, but enough that I notice, like leaving something out or having to double-check myself because I’m not sure if I wrote it down right.\n\nThat’s not really like me. I take that stuff seriously. So when I catch myself blanking out or losing my train of thought, it makes me feel even worse, because then I start thinking, “What is wrong with me? Why can’t I just focus?” It’s been frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed that, no. I mean… I feel slower inside, if that makes sense, like it takes me longer to get myself going or to sort through what I’m trying to do, but I’m not sure other people would say I’m moving slowly or talking slowly.\n\nAt work I’m still doing my normal tasks, walking around, helping people, answering questions. I haven’t had anyone ask me if I’m okay because I’m speaking weird or moving like I’m in slow motion or anything like that. Maybe I pause more because I’m trying to gather my thoughts, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually being slowed down.\n\nSo… not noticeably, I guess. Not that I can clearly point to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I wouldn’t say I have little or no pleasure in everything. It’s not like nothing feels good or I can’t enjoy anything at all.
